- [ ] **Step 7: The release workflow and its helper**

`.github/workflows/plugins-release.yml`:

```yaml
name: Plugins release

# A plugin is released on its own, by a tag `plugins/<id>-v<version>` that
# ./publish-plugin.sh pushes. Dexo's own release.yml is generated by cargo-dist and not
# touched by this.
on:
  push:
    tags: ["plugins/*-v*"]

permissions:
  contents: write

jobs:
  plan:
    runs-on: ubuntu-24.04
    outputs:
      id: ${{ steps.tag.outputs.id }}
      version: ${{ steps.tag.outputs.version }}
      kind: ${{ steps.tag.outputs.kind }}
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7.0.1
      - id: tag
        name: Read the tag
        run: |
          tag=${GITHUB_REF_NAME#plugins/}
          id=${tag%-v*}
          version=${tag##*-v}
          manifest=plugins/$id/dexo-plugin.toml
          [[ -f $manifest ]] || { echo "::error::no $manifest"; exit 1; }
          grep -qx "version = \"$version\"" "$manifest" || { echo "::error::$manifest is not at $version"; exit 1; }
          kind=$(sed -n 's/^kind = "\(.*\)"$/\1/p' "$manifest")
          {
            echo "id=$id"
            echo "version=$version"
            echo "kind=$kind"
          } >>"$GITHUB_OUTPUT"

  driver:
    needs: plan
    if: needs.plan.outputs.kind == 'driver'
    strategy:
      fail-fast: false
      matrix:
        include:
          - { target: x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu, os: ubuntu-22.04 }
          - { target: aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu, os: ubuntu-22.04-arm }
          - { target: x86_64-apple-darwin, os: macos-15-intel }
          - { target: aarch64-apple-darwin, os: macos-15 }
          - { target: x86_64-pc-windows-msvc, os: windows-2022 }
    runs-on: ${{ matrix.os }}
    env:
      ID: ${{ needs.plan.outputs.id }}
      VERSION: ${{ needs.plan.outputs.version }}
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7.0.1
      - uses: dtolnay/rust-toolchain@6c977a6ca4077a0ceb28ffbe03f59d46e9ac8772 # master
        with:
          toolchain: 1.93.1
      # DuckDB's C++ takes minutes to build on every platform: kept between releases.
      - uses: actions/cache@v4
        with:
          path: |
            ~/.cargo/registry
            ~/.cargo/git
            target
          key: plugin-${{ needs.plan.outputs.id }}-${{ matrix.target }}-${{ hashFiles('Cargo.lock') }}
          restore-keys: plugin-${{ needs.plan.outputs.id }}-${{ matrix.target }}-
      - name: Check the crate's version
        shell: bash
        run: |
          crate=crates/dexo-driver-$ID/Cargo.toml
          grep -qx "version = \"$VERSION\"" "$crate" || { echo "::error::$crate is not at $VERSION"; exit 1; }
      - name: Build
        shell: bash
        run: cargo build --release --locked -p "dexo-driver-$ID" --bin "dexo-driver-$ID"
      - name: Pack
        shell: bash
        run: |
          exe=target/release/dexo-driver-$ID
          [[ $RUNNER_OS == Windows ]] && exe=$exe.exe
          scripts/pack-plugin.sh "$ID" "$VERSION" "${{ matrix.target }}" "$exe"
      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v7
        with:
          name: plugin-${{ matrix.target }}
          path: dist/*.tar.gz

  theme:
    needs: plan
    if: needs.plan.outputs.kind == 'theme'
    runs-on: ubuntu-24.04
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7.0.1
      - name: Pack
        run: scripts/pack-plugin.sh "${{ needs.plan.outputs.id }}" "${{ needs.plan.outputs.version }}" any
      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v7
        with:
          name: plugin-any
          path: dist/*.tar.gz

  publish:
    needs: [plan, driver, theme]
    if: ${{ always() && needs.plan.result == 'success' && !contains(needs.*.result, 'failure') && !contains(needs.*.result, 'cancelled') }}
    runs-on: ubuntu-24.04
    # One index: two plugin releases at once would each upload their own.
    concurrency:
      group: plugins-index
      cancel-in-progress: false
    env:
      GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}
      ID: ${{ needs.plan.outputs.id }}
      VERSION: ${{ needs.plan.outputs.version }}
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7.0.1
      - uses: actions/download-artifact@v8
        with:
          pattern: plugin-*
          path: dist
          merge-multiple: true
      # Never "latest": Dexo's update check follows releases/latest to Dexo's own tag.
      - name: Release the artifacts
        run: |
          gh release create "plugins/$ID-v$VERSION" dist/*.tar.gz --latest=false \
            --title "Plugin $ID $VERSION" \
            --notes "The $ID plugin, $VERSION. Install it from Dexo's Plugins screen (Ctrl+G p) or with \`dexo plugin install $ID\`."
      - name: Add it to the index
        run: |
          mkdir -p current
          gh release download plugins-index --pattern index.json --dir current || true
          python3 scripts/plugin-index.py current/index.json "plugins/$ID/dexo-plugin.toml" dist \
            "https://github.com/$GITHUB_REPOSITORY/releases/download/plugins%2F$ID-v$VERSION" >index.json
      - name: Sign the index
        env:
          MINISIGN_SECRET_KEY: ${{ secrets.PLUGINS_MINISIGN_KEY }}
          MINISIGN_PASSWORD: ${{ secrets.PLUGINS_MINISIGN_PASSWORD }}
        run: |
          sudo apt-get update -qq && sudo apt-get install -y -qq minisign
          umask 077
          printf '%s\n' "$MINISIGN_SECRET_KEY" >"$RUNNER_TEMP/plugins.key"
          printf '%s\n' "$MINISIGN_PASSWORD" | minisign -S -s "$RUNNER_TEMP/plugins.key" -m index.json -t "Dexo plugin index"
          rm -f "$RUNNER_TEMP/plugins.key"
      - name: Publish the index
        run: |
          gh release view plugins-index >/dev/null 2>&1 ||
            gh release create plugins-index --latest=false --title "Plugin index" \
              --notes "The signed index of Dexo's plugins, read by Dexo."
          gh release upload plugins-index index.json index.json.minisig --clobber
```

`.github/workflows/integration.yml` — a job under `jobs:`:

```yaml
  duckdb-plugin:
    name: duckdb through its plugin executable
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7.0.1
      - uses: dtolnay/rust-toolchain@6c977a6ca4077a0ceb28ffbe03f59d46e9ac8772 # master
        with:
          toolchain: 1.93.1
      - name: DuckDB's contract through the plugin
        run: cargo test -p dexo-driver-duckdb --test through_plugin --test manifest
```

`publish-plugin.sh` at the repo root (`chmod +x`):

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Release a plugin: set its version in its manifest (and its crate, for a driver),
# commit, tag plugins/<id>-v<version> and push. The tag runs
# .github/workflows/plugins-release.yml, which builds, publishes and signs the index.
#
#   ./publish-plugin.sh <id> <version> [--dry-run]
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")"

die() {
  printf 'publish-plugin: %s\n' "$*" >&2
  exit 1
}

id=${1:-} version=${2:-}
dry_run=false
[[ ${3:-} == --dry-run ]] && dry_run=true
[[ -n $id && -n $version ]] || die "usage: ./publish-plugin.sh <id> <version> [--dry-run]"
[[ $version =~ ^[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+(-[0-9A-Za-z.-]+)?$ ]] || die "not a version: $version"
manifest=plugins/$id/dexo-plugin.toml
[[ -f $manifest ]] || die "no $manifest"
crate=crates/dexo-driver-$id/Cargo.toml
driver=false
grep -qx 'kind = "driver"' "$manifest" && driver=true
$driver && [[ ! -f $crate ]] && die "a driver plugin is built from $crate, which is missing"

[[ $(git branch --show-current) == main ]] || die "plugins are released from main"
[[ -z $(git status --porcelain --untracked-files=no) ]] || die "the working tree has uncommitted changes"
git fetch --quiet --tags origin
[[ $(git rev-parse HEAD) == "$(git rev-parse origin/main)" ]] ||
  die "main is not in sync with origin/main; pull or push first"
tag=plugins/$id-v$version
git rev-parse -q --verify "refs/tags/$tag" >/dev/null && die "tag $tag already exists"

if $dry_run; then
  echo "would set $id to $version, commit, and push $tag"
  exit 0
fi

# The first `version = ` line only: the package's, not a dependency's.
set_version() { perl -0pi -e 's/^version = "[^"]*"/version = "'"$version"'"/m' "$1"; }
set_version "$manifest"
files=("$manifest")
if $driver; then
  set_version "$crate"
  cargo metadata --format-version 1 >/dev/null # brings Cargo.lock along
  files+=("$crate" Cargo.lock)
fi
git commit -m "chore(plugins): $id $version" -- "${files[@]}"
git tag -a "$tag" -m "Plugin $id $version"
git push origin main "$tag"
```

Check it without a release: `./publish-plugin.sh duckdb 1.0.0 --dry-run`. On `development` it stops with "plugins are released from main", which is right.

- [ ] **Step 8: The docs**

`docs/src/plugins.md`:

````markdown
# Plugins

Plugins add to an installed Dexo without rebuilding it. There are two kinds:

- **Drivers** — a database engine, run as a program of its own. DuckDB is one.
- **Themes** — packs of colour themes.

## Using plugins

Open **Plugins** with `Ctrl+G p`, or from the command palette (*Plugins*, *Install Plugin…*, *Check Plugin Updates*).

- **Installed** lists what you have. **Available** lists what Dexo's plugin index offers that you do not have. Tab switches between them; `t` filters by type; `/` searches.
- A row's mark says its state: `●` enabled, `○` disabled, `↑` an update is available, `+` available to install. **local** marks a plugin installed from a file.
- The buttons act on the pick:
  - `i` installs.
  - `u` updates.
  - `e` enables or disables.
  - `a` applies a theme pack's first theme.
  - `x` removes, after asking.
- `r` fetches the index again; `f` installs from a file.
- While a download runs, a bar shows its progress, and Esc cancels it.

The same from the command line:

```sh
dexo plugin list [--available] [--json]
dexo plugin info duckdb [--json]
dexo plugin install duckdb [--version 1.0.0]
dexo plugin update duckdb        # or --all
dexo plugin disable duckdb       # keeps it installed, its drivers and themes left out
dexo plugin enable duckdb
dexo plugin remove duckdb --confirm
dexo plugin doctor [--json]
```

`dexo doctor` includes a one-line summary of the plugins.

A saved connection whose driver is a plugin you do not have stays saved. Opening it offers to install the plugin, and once it is in, the connection opens. The connection form lists such a driver as "DuckDB · not installed".

## Where plugins come from

Dexo's plugin index is a file published with every plugin release, signed with the maintainer's key. Dexo checks that signature against the keys built into it before it reads the index. Every download is then checked against the size and SHA-256 the index lists for it. A plugin from the index is marked **official, signed**.

Nothing is downloaded on its own. The index is fetched when you open the Plugins screen, when you run a `dexo plugin` command, and by the daily update check, which follows the **Check for updates** setting and `DEXO_NO_UPDATE_CHECK`. A fetched index is kept for a day. Offline, Dexo shows the last one it had and says from when.

If the signature does not match, Dexo says "the index signature does not match: nothing was installed", and installs nothing. There is no way past it: a mirror must serve an index signed by a key Dexo trusts.

## Installing from a file, a URL or a folder

```sh
dexo plugin install ./duckdb-1.0.0-x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu.tar.gz --trust
dexo plugin install https://example.com/my-themes-1.0.0.tar.gz --trust
dexo plugin install ./plugins/my-themes --trust     # a folder, for developing a plugin
```

Nothing vouches for a plugin that does not come from the index. Dexo shows its id, author and kind before it goes on. A driver plugin is a program that **will receive the passwords of the connections that use it**. The command line installs one only with `--trust`. The Plugins screen (`f`) asks you to type the plugin's id. Such plugins are marked **local, not verified** everywhere.

## Where plugins live

Under Dexo's data folder (`DEXO_DATA_HOME` moves it), in `plugins/`:

- `state.json` — what is installed, its version, origin and checksums.
- `index-cache.json` and `index-cache.json.minisig` — the last verified index.
- One folder per plugin.

An install unpacks into `plugins/.staging/` and moves into place at the end. A failure at any step leaves what was installed before. Two Dexos at once take turns through `plugins/.locks/`.

## A driver plugin's process

The process starts with the first connection that needs it and serves every connection of that plugin. It stops a minute after the last one closes, and when Dexo exits. It gets no `DEXO_*` variable and only `PATH`, the home and temporary folders and the locale from your environment. Connection passwords reach it inside the connection request, never in its arguments or environment.

If it crashes, the open connections say "the DuckDB plugin stopped" with the last thing it logged. The next connection starts it again, at most three times a minute.

Dexo keeps the SQL dialect, the statement guard and the production confirmations. They run before anything reaches a plugin, so a plugin driver cannot skip them.

## Environment variables

| Variable | What it does |
|---|---|
| `DEXO_PLUGIN_INDEX` | Fetches the index from this URL instead of Dexo's: a mirror, or a test. The signature is still required. |
| `DEXO_PLUGIN_TRUSTED_KEY` | Trusts one more minisign public key (its base64 line), for a mirror's or a test's index. |
| `DEXO_DRIVER_<ID>` | Runs this executable as plugin `<ID>` (uppercased, `-` as `_`) instead of the installed one: `DEXO_DRIVER_DUCKDB=target/debug/dexo-driver-duckdb`. For developing a driver. |

## The package format

A plugin is a `.tar.gz` holding:
- `dexo-plugin.toml`, and optionally `LICENSE` and `README.md`;
- for a driver, its executable directly under `bin/`;
- for a theme pack, its themes directly under `themes/`.

Anything else is refused, as are links, absolute paths and `..`. A driver ships one archive per platform, named `<id>-<version>-<target>.tar.gz`. A theme pack ships one, `<id>-<version>.tar.gz`.

```toml
schema = 1                      # the manifest format's version
id = "duckdb"                   # 2 to 32 of a-z, 0-9 and -, starting with a letter or digit
kind = "driver"                 # "driver" or "theme"
version = "1.0.0"
name = "DuckDB"
description = "Open DuckDB files, CSV, Parquet and JSON"
authors = ["KingDasWinx"]
license = "MIT OR Apache-2.0"
homepage = "https://github.com/KingDasWinx/Dexo"
min_dexo = "1.5.0"              # the oldest Dexo it runs on

[driver]
protocol = 1                    # the plugin protocol version it speaks
drivers = ["duckdb"]            # the connection driver ids it provides
display_name = "DuckDB"
dialect = "duckdb"              # a dialect Dexo knows; any other gets generic SQL handling
file = true                     # a file database: the form asks for a path, not a host
url_schemes = ["duckdb"]
executable = "bin/dexo-driver-duckdb"   # .exe is added on Windows

# or, for a theme pack:
# [theme]
# themes = ["themes/*.toml"]
```

An unknown key is an error. A theme file takes the format of [a theme of your own](workbench.md).

## Writing a driver plugin

A driver plugin is any program that speaks the plugin protocol on stdin and stdout. In Rust, the SDK turns a `ConnectionFactory` into one:

```rust
fn main() {
    dexo_plugin_sdk::serve(
        vec![std::sync::Arc::new(my_driver::MyFactory)],
        env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"),
    );
}
```

`serve` returns when stdin closes. While you work on it, run it with `DEXO_DRIVER_<ID>` pointing at your build.

### The protocol

JSON-RPC 2.0, one message per line. Dexo sends requests; the plugin answers each by its `id`, in any order. stdout carries only the protocol; stderr is the plugin's log, whose last lines Dexo shows when it fails. A line over 64 MB is an error.

1. **Handshake.**
   - Dexo sends `initialize`: `{"dexo_version": "1.5.0", "protocols": [1]}`.
   - The plugin answers `{"protocol": 1, "driver_version": "1.0.0", "drivers": ["duckdb"]}` within 10 seconds, naming a protocol it was offered and every driver its manifest declares.
2. **Sessions.**
   - `session/open` carries the connection request, with its password and other secrets.
   - It answers `{"session", "capabilities", "families", "server_session_id"?, "transaction"?}`. `families` lists what the session answers beyond queries: `catalog`, `data`, `bulk`, `explain`, `transactions`, `cancel`.
   - `session/close` ends a session.
3. **Calls on a session** take `{"session": "s1", "args": ...}`.

| Family | Methods |
|---|---|
| query | `query/execute`, `query/fetch`, `query/close`, `query/cancel` |
| catalog | `catalog/list_children`, `catalog/object`, `catalog/ddl`, `catalog/dependencies`, `catalog/dependents`, `catalog/foreign_keys`, `catalog/relations_named`, `catalog/databases` |
| data | `data/fetch`, `data/fetch_value`, `data/apply`, `data/table_columns`, `data/estimate_rows` |
| bulk | `bulk/insert_batch` |
| explain | `explain/explain` |
| transactions | `tx/begin`, `tx/commit`, `tx/rollback`, `tx/savepoint`, `tx/rollback_to`, `tx/release_savepoint` — each answers `{"state", "notice"?, "error"?}` |

A result comes in pages the host asks for:
- `query/execute` answers the first page and a cursor.
- `query/fetch {"cursor"}` answers the next.
- `query/close` drops a cursor that is not finished.
- A page holds up to 500 rows or about 4 MB.
- The last page has `"done": true`, the session's transaction state, and an error if the query failed.

`query/cancel` is answered while a query runs. A plugin that does not answer it within 5 seconds is stopped.

Values travel tagged, `{"t": "i64", "v": 1}`; bytes are base64. A driver's error travels in the JSON-RPC error's `data`, with its category, code, position and hints. The types and method names are in the `dexo-plugin-protocol` crate.

## For maintainers

### The signing key

Done once, by the maintainer, on their own machine:

1. `minisign -G -p dexo-plugins.pub -s dexo-plugins.key`, with a strong password. Make a second pair the same way, `dexo-plugins-next`, and keep it offline: it is the next key.
2. In the repository's GitHub settings, store the contents of `dexo-plugins.key` as the secret `PLUGINS_MINISIGN_KEY`, and its password as `PLUGINS_MINISIGN_PASSWORD`.
3. Put both public keys — the second line of each `.pub` — in `crates/dexo-app/src/plugin/keys.rs`.

The private keys never enter the repository, a chat or an issue. To rotate, sign with the next key, and ship a Dexo that lists a new next key.

### Releasing a plugin

From a clean, pushed `main`: `./publish-plugin.sh duckdb 1.0.1`. It sets the version in `plugins/duckdb/dexo-plugin.toml` and the driver's crate, commits, and pushes the tag `plugins/duckdb-v1.0.1`.

`plugins-release.yml` then:
1. Builds the driver on each platform.
2. Publishes a release with the archives.
3. Adds them to the index with `scripts/plugin-index.py`.
4. Signs the index and replaces it on the `plugins-index` release.
````

```markdown
A driver whose dependencies are heavy ships as a plugin instead of in Dexo: DuckDB's crate builds a `dexo-driver-duckdb` executable whose `main` hands its `ConnectionFactory` to `dexo_plugin_sdk::serve`. Dexo runs it through `PluginFactory`, which implements the driver traits over the plugin protocol ([Plugins](plugins.md)). `DriverDescriptor::for_id` still knows the driver, so its connections are read, and the dialect and the statement guard stay in Dexo. Its tests run twice: in process (`tests/driver.rs`) and through the executable (`tests/through_plugin.rs`). SQLite's run through the SDK and the proxy in process the same way, to keep the protocol whole.

The workspace's clippy and test runs build every crate, DuckDB's engine included, on each CI platform: each job spends minutes compiling its C++ before any Rust. `cargo test -p <crate>` skips it locally for work that does not touch DuckDB.
```

- [ ] **Step 2: A signing tool for QA**

`crates/dexo-test-support/examples/sign_index.rs`:

```rust
//! Signs a file with a fresh test key, for trying a local plugin index by hand:
//! `cargo run -p dexo-test-support --example sign_index -- index.json` writes
//! `index.json.minisig` and prints the public key for `DEXO_PLUGIN_TRUSTED_KEY`.
fn main() {
    let path = std::env::args().nth(1).expect("the file to sign");
    let key = dexo_test_support::plugins::TestKey::new();
    let data = std::fs::read(&path).expect("the file");
    std::fs::write(format!("{path}.minisig"), key.sign(&data)).expect("the signature");
    println!("{}", key.public());
}
```

1. Set up a scratch home, build, and pack a QA copy of DuckDB with `min_dexo` at the running version (the real manifest says 1.5.0, and this build is still 1.4.x):

```bash
sed -i "s/^min_dexo = .*/min_dexo = \"$(cargo pkgid -p dexo | sed 's/.*[#@]//')\"/" $QA/plugins/*/dexo-plugin.toml
```

2. Pack both from `$QA/plugins`: run `scripts/pack-plugin.sh` from a scratch copy of the repo root, or temporarily point `source_dir` at `$QA/plugins/$id`.
3. Build `index.json` with `scripts/plugin-index.py` and the URL `http://127.0.0.1:8765`.
4. Sign it with `cargo run -p dexo-test-support --example sign_index -- $QA/dist/index.json`.
5. Serve it: `python3 -m http.server 8765 --directory $QA/dist &`.
6. Run `DEXO_PLUGIN_INDEX=http://127.0.0.1:8765/index.json DEXO_PLUGIN_TRUSTED_KEY=<printed key> ./target/debug/dexo` in tmux at 120×35, and check each of these:
   - `Ctrl+G p` opens Plugins; the top line reads `… History  Plugins`. Installed is empty and says so; Tab shows DuckDB and Extra themes, `+` marked.
   - On DuckDB, `i` shows the progress bar, then a toast, and the row moves to Installed with `●`.
   - New connection: the driver list shows DuckDB without "· not installed". Connect `:memory:` and run `select 42`: the answer is 42.

   - Stop the HTTP server and press `r`: "offline: showing the index from <today>".

7. CLI against the same index: `dexo plugin list --available`, `install duckdb`, `info duckdb --json`, `doctor`, `remove duckdb --confirm`.
